On this machine (2× EPYC 7763, 256 hardware threads), the 512³ grid with 20 iterations dropped from **6749 ms to about 220 ms**, roughly 30x faster. The output is bit-for-bit identical to the original: the hash matched on the large grid and on edge cases including grids of size 1, 2 and 3, non-cubic and odd sizes, and 0 iterations. Validation passes.\n\nOnly the two existing files changed:\n\n**`CMakeLists.txt`**\n- Adds `find_package(OpenMP REQUIRED)` and links `OpenMP::OpenMP_CXX`.\n- Same executable name and compile flags. No fast-math, so floating-point results don't change.\n\n**`stencil3d.cpp`**\n- **One parallel region for all iterations.** The threads are created once and split the grid rows evenly between them. The boundary copy is folded into the same pass, so each iteration reads the input grid only once.\n- **Memory placed next to the threads that use it.** The grids are no longer zero-filled by a single thread when they're created. Each thread fills its own rows in a parallel initialization, so those pages land on the thread's own memory node.\n- **Thread pinning.** Without it, threads moved between CPUs and runs were about 4x slower. When you haven't set `OMP_PROC_BIND` or `OMP_PLACES`, the program pins each thread to its own physical core, spread across both sockets. It does this with the standard Linux `sched_setaffinity` call and reads the core layout from `/sys`, so there are no new dependencies. If you set those variables, the program leaves binding to OpenMP.\n- **Vectorized inner loop.** The arithmetic is unchanged: same addition order, same division by 7.0.\n- **Streaming stores for large grids only.** These are AVX writes that bypass the cache. They're used only when the two grids total more than 1 GiB. That threshold is tuned for this machine's 512 MB of total L3 cache; smaller cache-resident grids run about 2x slower with them. At 512³ they gave about 23% more speed, and code built without AVX falls back to the plain loop.\n- **Validation checks** (NaN/Inf, min/max) now also run in parallel.\n\nTwo things to be aware of:\n- **Speed stops improving after about 16 threads.** At 512³ the run takes 228 ms at 16 threads and 217–224 ms at 64 or 256. Memory bandwidth is the limit, not the number of cores. Going further would need a different algorithm, such as processing several iterations per pass over memory, which I didn't do.\n- **`-r` makes a temporary copy.** The shared `print_results` helper only accepts a standard vector, so the program copies the final grid before printing.
